--- Achtung! Cthulhu (setting).txt ---

[1] Zoth L181
Cthulhu Cult : Which has its roots in the Pacific Ocean. It consists of Deep Ones and their human allies, who have been seduced or bribed with promises of wealth and/or power. Their ultimate objective is to free Cthulhu . In the meantime they increase their numbers by breeding with humans (willingly or unwillingly) and spread their influence. Often working with the Cthulhu Cults are the Star-Spawn that still remain on Earth. They also are trying to free the Sons of Cthulhu ( Ythogtha Ghatanothoa , and Zoth-Ommog ), all of whom are imprisoned in the Pacific Ocean. Nyarlathotep : It has cults across the world, each of which is dedicated to a different incarnation of the god. The main cults are in China, Australia, Kenya, Egypt , Mexico, and in the United States. Thousand Sons : An organization founded by the ancient race, the Naga. Their last great city was Hsatth, in Korea where they retreated to and sealed themselves in the underground city. A dozen Naga (the last remaining ones on Earth) rule over the Serpent People.


--- Achtung!_Cthulhu_(setting)_The_H.P._Lovecraft_Wiki_Fandom_f32b58fea5.txt ---

[1] Zoth L283
Dedicated to the exploitation of lost technology and using it to create ever more powerful and fearsome machines of war. Cthulhu Cult: Which has its roots in the Pacific Ocean. It consists of Deep Ones and their human allies, who have been seduced or bribed with promises of wealth and/or power. Their ultimate objective is to free Cthulhu. In the meantime they increase their numbers by breeding with humans (willingly or unwillingly) and spread their influence. Often working with the Cthulhu Cults are the Star-Spawn that still remain on Earth. They also are trying to free the Sons of Cthulhu (Ythogtha, Ghatanothoa, and Zoth-Ommog), all of whom are imprisoned in the Pacific Ocean. Nyarlathotep: It has cults across the world, each of which is dedicated to a different incarnation of the god. The main cults are in China, Australia, Kenya, Egypt, Mexico, and in the United States. Thousand Sons: An organization founded by the ancient race, the Naga. Their last great city was Hsatth, in Korea where they retreated to and sealed themselves in the underground city. A dozen Naga (the last remaining ones on Earth) rule over the Serpent People.


--- Aldebaran.txt ---

[1] Dark Star L127
P. Lovecraft . The unnamed narrator observed Aldebaran from the city of Olathoë in the Arctic region of Lomar in the Dreamlands . The red star never set but crawled low around the horizon during the extended polar night. Aldebaran is visible in the sky above Kingsport during the events of " The Festival " by H. P. Lovecraft, making the narrator shiver as the star seems to balance itself on the spire of the church. The Byakhee , which debuted in that story, are revealed to be servants of Hastur in Derleth's The Trail of Cthulhu Overview Also known as the Eye of the Bull, Aldebaran lies in the constellation of Taurus and is said to be the star of the Great Old One Hastur, who inhabits the Lake of Hali on a planet circling a nearby dark star. The Celaeno Library might also be located here (although it should be noted that Celaeno is a different star).

--- Azathoth.txt ---

[8] daemon-sultan [fuzzy:86%] L141
Lovecraft There were, in such voyages, incalculable local dangers; as well as that shocking final peril which gibbers unmentionably outside the ordered universe, where no dreams reach; that last amorphous blight of nethermost confusion which blasphemes and bubbles at the centre of all infinity—the boundless daemon-sultan Azathoth, whose name no lips dare speak aloud, and who gnaws hungrily in inconceivable, unlighted chambers beyond time amidst the muffled, maddening beating of vile drums and the thin, monotonous whine of accursed flutes; to which detestable pounding and piping dance slowly, awkwardly, and absurdly the gigantic ultimate gods, the blind, voiceless, tenebrous, mindless Other Gods whose soul and messenger is the crawling chaos Nyarlathotep HPL The Dream-Quest of Unknown Kadath Azathoth , sometimes called " The Blind Idiot God ", the " Nuclear Chaos ", and the " Daemon Sultan ", is a fictional deity in the Cthulhu Mythos --portrayed as an Outer God and a supreme omnipotent being.

--- Celaeno (Cthulhu Universalis).txt ---

[1] Dark Star L145
This summoned the bat-winged byakhee , who transported them to the Nameless City in Arabia . There, they left their bodies in hibernation while their astral selves traveled to Celaeno. It is not clear how their astral selves were transported to Celaeno, though this may also have involved using the byakhee. [ AWD Curwen Sky Hastur and the Hyades Claiborne Boyd and Abel Keane each mistakenly believed that Celaeno is in the Hyades , which is another star cluster in the constellation Taurus. Boyd jumped to this conclusion after reading a reference to a dark star in the Hyades in the R'lyeh Text ; since Boyd had recently heard of Celaeno, he made the leap of connecting the two. [ AWD Sky ] Abel Keane had a vision of Laban Shrewsbury , in which he was supposedly told that Celaeno is "a place far removed from our universe—Celaeno in the Hyades" [ AWD Gorge ]. This lapse can perhaps be explained if you suppose that Shrewsbury referred to Celaeno and the Pleiades as being somehow related to the Hyades, which is the realm of Hastur. Together with the use of Hastur's byakhee for transport, these factors seem to imply that Celaeno is controlled by servants of Hastur.

--- Cone-Creatures (Cthulhu Universalis).txt ---

[1] Dark Star L25
НАЗВАНИЕ: Cone-Creatures (Cthulhu Universalis) ИСТОЧНИК: Cone-Creatures A race that peopled earth about a billion years ago. They belonged to no surviving, or scientifically known, line of terrestrial evolution. Their minds were displaced by the minds of the Great Race from the planet Yith about 600 million years ago. [ HPL Time online text )] Other populations of cone creatures also exist on a dark star in Taurus [ AWD Space ] and on a planet in another universe AWD Brotherhood . Both groups are possessed by the Great Race. For further information on the cone-creatures, see the article on the Great Race Return to Cthulhu Universalis Contents Page Return to CthulhuFiles. com Home Page Send comments to

--- Cthaeghya.txt ---

[4] Zoth L121
Cthaeghya and her family journeyed to Yuggoth , where Rahb mated with the infertile Cthaeghya. They were forced to flee when Cxaxukluth began feeding off its progeny, with Cthaeghya and Rahb fleeing to Xoth . There, Rahb began mating with Idh-yaa , who bore his three sons: Ghatanothoa Ythogtha and Zoth-Ommog . In a fit of rage, Cthaeghya destroyed Idh-yaa and then headed towards Magmhel. During their attack on Xoth, the Elder Beings managed to kidnap Rahb, with his sons escaping to Magmhel in order to tell Cthaeghya about their father's capture. Journeying to Xoth, Cthaeghya successfully freed Rahb; however, the servitors of the Elder Beings followed them back to Magmhel, leading to the destruction of the planet and Rahb being caught again, with Cthaeghya and the Sons of Rahb fleeing to nearby Aihai .


--- Cthulhi.txt ---

[1] Zoth L251
Conversely, in The Transition of Titus Crow , it is strongly implied that the Cthulhi were already present on Earth when the moon was ripped off from the Pacific Ocean , taking some of their settlements. As the Cthulhi are known to affect the minds of other beings, their presence in the moon accounts for its historical association with insanity (as in the word "lunacy"), and is also the reason why dogs and wolves howl at the moon. Lin Carter, in his Xothic Legend Cycle , uses the term "Spawn of Cthulhu" to refer to the three sons of Cthulhu and Idh-yaa Ghatanothoa Ythogtha , and Zoth-Ommog . Carter rarely refers to the actual Cthulhi species in his fiction, although he does refer to them in the "Papyrus of the Dark Wisdom", revealing that, like the three sons mentioned above, the Cthulhi are also from Xoth. It's not known whether they are the children of Cthulhu and Idh-yaa as well. In the Worlds of H. P. Lovecraft comic book series, the "Spawn of Cthulhu" are actually the Deep Ones , and the white apes are merely an intermediary stage in the transformation of humans into Deep Ones.

--- Cthulhu_The_H.P._Lovecraft_Wiki_Fandom_7fd7f6373c.txt ---

[17] Zoth L663
Before he came to our Solar System, Cthulhu mated with Idh-yaa on the planet Xoth. His offspring are Ghatanothoa, Ythogtha, Zoth-Ommog (EXP: "The Horror in the Gallery"), and Cthylla (EXP: The Transition of Titus Crow). The Star-Spawn were also sired by Cthulhu on Xoth (EXP: "Papyrus of the Dark Wisdom").

--- Danny O'Bannion.txt ---

[1] Dark Star L65
НАЗВАНИЕ: Danny O'Bannion ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:15:35 СИМВОЛОВ: 1935 Danny O'Bannion Origin Arkham Massachusetts Offspring Naomi O'Bannion (daughter) Occupation Criminal Affiliations The Syndicate Species Human First appearance EXP Arkham Unveiled Created by Keith Herber Danny O'Bannion is a fictional character created by Keith Herber for the first edition of Arkham Unveiled , published by Chaosium in 1990 for the Call of Cthulhu role-playing game, where he is presented as the owner of Lucky Clover Cartage Company, a front for O'Bannion's bootlegging operations. O'Bannion's name is Herber's homage to the American screenwriter and director Dan O'Bannon (who co-wrote the screenplay of Dark Star (1974) and wrote the screenplay of Alien (1979) among other films), according to correspondence between Herber and fellow Call of Cthulhu writer Richard Watts during the development of Arkham Unveiled . Most recently, O'Bannion reappears in Chaosium's Arkham (2023), the first of several releases planned for the 'Arkham Unveiled' line (formerly known as 'Lovecraft Country') for the 7th edition of the Call of Cthulhu roleplaying game. O'Bannion has also appeared in publications from Fantasy Flight Games (establised 1995) for their Arkham Horror Files series of Cthulhu Mythos -related releases. Although primarily appearing in the now-discontinued Call of Cthulhu Living Card Game, he has also been referenced in other media, such as Peter J.

In the Year of the Red Moon, Ghatanothoa becomes the supreme god in Mu, following the god's destruction of a high priest of Shub-Niggurath.

--- Events in H. P. Lovecraft's fiction.txt ---

[4] Dark Star L1373
D. Pickman Carter uses strange means to repel the Mongol hordes from Australia. 2518 A. D. Australian physicist Nevil Kingston-Brown, who mindswapped with a Yithian, dies. 5000 A. D. ca Philosopher Yiang-Li from the cruel empire of Tsan-Chan mindswaps with a Yithian. 16,000 A. D. Nug-Soth , a magician of the dark conquerors, mindswaps with a Yithian. 18,000,000 A. D. A half-plastic denizen of the hollow interior of a trans-Plutonian planet mindswaps with a Yithian. Unknown Time Following the final end of humanity, a new race of beetle-like insects arises. These are possessed by the Great Race of Yith, leaving their previous home on a planet orbiting a dark star near Taurus. Eventually, even the beetles die out, and the Yithians send their collective minds into a vegetable species living on Mercury. The first primitive life develops on Venus . One Venusian is among those that exchanges minds with the Great Race of Yith. The last inhabitants of Earth, a species of arachnids, live within the interior of the dying world.

[2] Zoth L61
They were believed to worship gods or devils with names like "Zhar" and "Lloigor," but so little about them was known for certain, that many authorities seemed to consider them to be merely legendary. I was also to search for any and every reference to Leng itself; to a certain Tcho-Tcho lama who veiled his visage behind a mask of yellow silk and dwelt in a "prehistoric" stone monastery; to Inquanok, which seemed to be both a people and a place, the place being adjacent to the plateau of Leng; and to certain sea-divinities or maritime demons with uncouth names like "Cthulhu," "Idh-yaa," "Zoth-Ommog," "Yeb," "Ghatanothoa," "Ubb, Father of Worms," "Ythogtha," and so on. None of this research was particularly demanding of my time, but it was oddly disturbing.

--- Great_Race_(Cthulhu_Universalis)_20605ea143 [Great Race (Cthulhu Universalis).txt].txt ---

[1] Dark Star L109
The difference can be resolved by referring to the Necronomicon quote given previously, which specifies that the Elder Things are members or allies of the Great Old Ones. According to Piper, the Great Race have always striven for solitude and peace, but are more closely akin to the Elder Gods than to the Great Old Ones. In Peaslee's version, the Great Race migrated to the future coleopterous race on earth, and will migrate again to the bodies of bulbous vegetable entities of Mercury. In Piper's version, The Great Race fled from Earth to Jupiter, and then to a planet distantly orbiting a dark star in Taurus. There, they occupied the bodies of another population of cone-creatures, identical in appearance to the cone-creatures that they previously inhabited on Earth. Amos Piper dwelt with them in one of the cone-creature bodies during his captivity. They remain watchful for invasion from the region of the Lake of Hali, where Hastur was banished by the Elder Gods. Their star is dying, and they are preparing for a mass migration to another star, either backward or forward in time.

--- Henry Stephenson Blaine.txt ---

[1] Zoth L79
D. , was the curator of the Manuscripts Collection at the Sanbourne Institute of Pacific Antiquities in Santiago, California ( EXP The Dweller in the Tomb "). He once studied Semitic Languages under George Gammell Angell EXP Out of the Ages In 1928, Dr. Blaine was in charge of making an inventory of the collection of artifacts and manuscripts that formerly belonged to the late Prof. Harold Hadley Copeland . Among Copeland's possessions was a carved jade idol of Zoth-Ommog and a copy of the Yuggya Chants . The idol gave Blaine nightmares and affected his mind, showing him images of the submerged Zoth-Ommog and trying to influence Blaine to remove the Elder Sign that kept the god imprisoned. At night, Blaine was afflicted with episodes of somnambulism in which he would walk to the beach at Wexton Pier with a paper containing an incantation copied from the Yuggya Chants . One night, he was found sleepwalking into the sea.

--- Idh-yaa.txt ---

[1] Zoth L21
НАЗВАНИЕ: Idh-yaa ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:21:27 СИМВОЛОВ: 628 Idh-yaa Origin Outer space Offspring Ghatanothoa Ythogtha Zoth-Ommog Cthylla Mate Cthulhu Species Great Old Ones First appearance EXP Out of the Ages Created by Lin Carter Idh-yaa The Mighty Mother , is a Great Old One in the expanded Cthulhu Mythos . The character was originally created by Lin Carter for his short story " Out of the Ages Overview Idh-yaa is a deity who gave birth to the Star-Spawn of Cthulhu on a planet in the Xoth Star system, after mating with Cthulhu . She specifically produced the sons of Cthulhu: Ghatanothoa Ythogtha , and Zoth-Ommog ; and a daughter of Cthulhu: Cthylla . Also once called Quum-yaa.

--- Karter_Za-maskoy.XZgBpQ.561690.txt ---

[8] Зот L169
Глоссарий Таттла оказался ошибочным, и многие из значений, которые Татлл записал, Хоскинс заменил на более правильные определения... хотя, как он сам пришел к ним, даже он не мог сказать. Возможно, это была интуиция. Постепенно, однако, смысловые образы, сокрытые в таинственном языке, начали появляться. "Текст" был смесью песнопений и молитв, литаний и заклинаний для нескольких божеств, которые носили такие имена, как Ктулху, Идх-йаа, Зот-Оммог, Убб, Гхатанотоа, Йтхогта, Дагон, Гидра и Йеб. [9] Зот L169
"Текст" был смесью песнопений и молитв, литаний и заклинаний для нескольких божеств, которые носили такие имена, как Ктулху, Идх-йаа, Зот-Оммог, Убб, Гхатанотоа, Йтхогта, Дагон, Гидра и Йеб. Все они, как было понятно из текста, стихийные духи вод земли, демоны-боги океана. Они были, как он понял, заключены в тюрьмы на разных участках океанского дна океана — Ктулху в затонувшем каменном городе Р'льех, его сын Йтхогта в Йхе, Зот-Оммог в подводной пропасти возле "Острова каменных городов" (термин этот, который по какой-то причине снова, возможно, интуиция помогла вспомнить Хоскинсу, относился к острову Понапе с его колоссальными развалинами Нан-Матала) и Гхатанотоа в запечатанном склепе в погруженной на дно горе Йаддит-Гхо. Каждому из этих морских демонов служили племена приспешников-миньонов, Ктулху — глубоководные, лидерами которых были "Отец Дагон и Мать Гидра" — имена, конечно, знакомые Хоскинсу из более ранних прочтений. Например, Дагон был морским богом филистимлян, а Гидра — богиня-монстр из греческой мифологии.

[10] Зот L171
Каждому из этих морских демонов служили племена приспешников-миньонов, Ктулху — глубоководные, лидерами которых были "Отец Дагон и Мать Гидра" — имена, конечно, знакомые Хоскинсу из более ранних прочтений. Например, Дагон был морским богом филистимлян, а Гидра — богиня-монстр из греческой мифологии. С другой стороны, Йтхогту и Зот-Оммога обслуживала раса под названием Йугг, лидером которой был Убб, "Отец Червей"; у Гхатанотоа были темные слуги, их лидером был Йеб. Эти трое последних были, судя по всему, сыновьями (или "отродьями", как говорилось в "Тексте Р'льех") самого Ктулху; сыновьями, которых он породил многие эоны назад от последнего члена этого мрачного пантеона, Идх-йаа.

--- Out of the Ages.txt ---

[1] Zoth L83
It is part of Carter's Xothic Legend Cycle Synopsis The bulk of the narrative consists of several entries from the diary of Dr. Henry Stephenson Blaine , curator of the manuscripts collection at the Sanbourne Institute of Pacific Antiquities in Santiago, California. In 1928, Dr. Blaine has been tasked with cataloging the objects and documents of the Copeland Collection, formerly belonging to the late Professor Harold Hadley Copeland , who died screaming in a mental institution two years before. In the Copeland Collection, Blaine finds several statuettes and ceremonial masks from widely separated cultures across the Pacific , all seemingly depicting the same aquatic deity, known by names such as Zatamagwa Sothamogha or Z'mog , which according to Copeland's annotations, are all variations of Zoth-Ommog . The main artifact is a skillfully sculpted jade figurine found by a diver off the coast of Ponape, which depicts Zoth-Ommog himself: a creature that combines aspects of starfish, squid and reptile. By reading Copeland's papers, Blaine finds evidence of a prehistoric civilization in ancient Mu which worshiped the Xothic gods, sons of Cthulhu , who were served by slug-like creatures called Yuggya . There is also record of a 19th century ship whose crew was attacked by the Yuggya, suggesting that the creatures remain active even today.

[2] Zoth L85
In 1928, Dr. Blaine has been tasked with cataloging the objects and documents of the Copeland Collection, formerly belonging to the late Professor Harold Hadley Copeland , who died screaming in a mental institution two years before. In the Copeland Collection, Blaine finds several statuettes and ceremonial masks from widely separated cultures across the Pacific , all seemingly depicting the same aquatic deity, known by names such as Zatamagwa Sothamogha or Z'mog , which according to Copeland's annotations, are all variations of Zoth-Ommog . The main artifact is a skillfully sculpted jade figurine found by a diver off the coast of Ponape, which depicts Zoth-Ommog himself: a creature that combines aspects of starfish, squid and reptile. By reading Copeland's papers, Blaine finds evidence of a prehistoric civilization in ancient Mu which worshiped the Xothic gods, sons of Cthulhu , who were served by slug-like creatures called Yuggya . There is also record of a 19th century ship whose crew was attacked by the Yuggya, suggesting that the creatures remain active even today.

--- R'lyeh Text.txt ---

[1] Zoth L131
And there, only a paragraph below, appeared what purported to be a translation of the utterly unknown language of the Text: "In his house at R'lyeh , dead Cthulhu waits dreaming". AWD The Return of Hastur The R'lyeh Text is a fictional grimoire created by August Derleth . It first appeared in his short story " The Return of Hastur " and was later featured in many other works of the expanded Cthulhu Mythos , including Lin Carter Xothic Legend Cycle Written by author or authors unknown, the R'lyeh Text contains information on Cthulhu Ghatanothoa Zoth-Ommog Cyäegha and other Great Old Ones Known Copies In August Derleth's short story "The Return of Hastur", Amos Tuttle paid a hundred thousand dollars for a copy of the R'lyeh Text that came from "the dark interior of Asia". The book was bound in human skin. In Lin Carter's short stories " Behind the Mask " and " The Strange Doom of Enos Harker ", a translation of the R'lyeh Text was produced by Bryant Hoskins based on the aforementioned Tuttle copy, and kept in the library of Miskatonic University In Brian Lumley 's short story " Lord of the Worms ", Julian Carstairs has a copy that he keeps in his library at The Barrows. In Robert M.

--- Siren.txt ---

[1] Zoth L59
Traditionally, they have been depicted as woman-headed birds who inhabit rocky islands and cliffs. The Sirens first appear in the Homeric epic poem, the Odyssey They were traditionally believed to be sired by Phorcys who mated with the sea monster Ceto, immortal creatures whom use their voice to lure men to their deaths. According to one source, the Syrens were banished from the Earth 's surface in ancient times and exiled to the abyssal depths of the oceans, where their descendants became known as the Spawn of the Green Abyss , ruled by their queen Zoth Syra ADJ Spawn of the Green Abyss In Marvel Comics In the Marvel Multiverse , Sirens are the descendants of the Elder God known as Set . The Marvel Comics incarnation of these creatures were created in 1970 by Roy Thomas and Sal Buscema, making their first appearance in issue #29 of the 1st volume of the Sub-Mariner comic book series. In the Call of Cthulhu RPG Siren, by Reuben Dodd Cthulhu Invictus Sirens are a species of winged bird-like humanoids who originated on the planet that stood between Mars and Jupiter. When their homeworld was destroyed and turned into the Asteroid Belt, the Sirens fled to Earth.

--- Spawn of the Green Abyss.txt ---

[1] Zoth L67
Real World Information First appearance ADJ Spawn of the Green Abyss Appearances EXP Ye Booke of Monstres Created by C. Hall Thompson The Spawn of the Green Abyss are a fictional species created by C. Hall Thompson for his eponymous short story . They were later incorperated into Chaosium 's role-playing game Call of Cthulhu EXP Ye Booke of Monstres Background The Spawn of the Green Abyss are a highly evolved and intelligent race which lives in the oceans and seas of Earth. Living in societies ruled by their queen, Zoth Syra. They appear as bluish slime whose various features are shifting. Limbs and sensory organs are created as needed. The songs of the spawn can influence those whom hear it. Though normal weapons cannot hurt them, they can be harmed by fire, chemicals, electricity, magical spells, and enchanted weapons. The Spawn of the Green Abyss herald from outer space and came to this planet long ago to inhabit oceans and watery places.

[2] Zoth L73
The Spawn of the Green Abyss herald from outer space and came to this planet long ago to inhabit oceans and watery places. While similar to the Shoggoths , in some respects, the spawn are utterly alien and possess great intelligence, sometimes traveling in groups for undecipherable reasons. They tend to remain in deep waters, occasionally swimming to the surface to hold what appear to be strange rituals when certain cosmic alignments take place. Some fishermen—who claimed to have been taken down into the depths by the spawn—speak of the monsters dwelling in bizarre aquatic societies and that they are ruled by beings named Zoth Syra and with Kala. Some have alleged the spawn are actually part of Deep One society and can be found deep within that race’s underwater cities. The spawn appears as a bluish-black slime with various and shifting features, able to create limbs and sensory organs as needed. They are capable of speech, including human tongue, and use a form of "singing" that, akin to old sea tales of mermaid and siren song, is able to command the will of weak-willed folk.

--- Stories_d0e3dd6240 [Donald Wandrei, The Fire Vampires.txt].txt ---

[2] Dark Star L227
Who could predict what would happen next? A strange hush brooded over the great cities, throughout a sultry day. When the shadows of twilight darkened on the ground, men went out singly and in groups, by millions, to watch the newcomer. And long before darkness came, long before the first star shone, Norby’s comet blazed forth bluish and brilliant, terribly near in the zenith, its tail sweeping fanwise in a majestic but awe-inspiring streamer of tremendous length. It was a marvel of the heavens, an omen, a prodigy that men compared to the Dark Star which had swept out of space in the Twentieth Century. Would it wreck the earth? Would it plunge onward in its wild rush and crash flaming down upon a continent? Would it weld with the sun and cause who knew what disasters of collision or immeasurable heat? Humanity shivered at the terrifying and menacing beauty of the comet—so near, so dreadfully near, and so radiant that all the stars were outshone, and the moon gleamed wan and feeble.

There were, in such voyages, incalculable local dangers; as well as that shocking final peril which gibbers unmentionably outside the ordered universe, where no dreams reach; that last amorphous blight of nethermost confusion which blasphemes and bubbles at the centre of all infinity—the boundless daemon-sultan Azathoth, whose name no lips dare speak aloud, and who gnaws hungrily in inconceivable, unlighted chambers beyond time amidst the muffled, maddening beating of vile drums and the thin, monotonous whine of accursed flutes; to which detestable pounding and piping dance slowly, awkwardly, and absurdly the gigantic ultimate gods, the blind, voiceless, tenebrous, mindless Other Gods whose soul and messenger is the crawling chaos Nyarlathotep.

И все же ужасный звезднорожденный вампир, казалось, лишь мерцал, то появляясь, то исчезая из виду, пока жизненная сущность Зизоты пульсировала сквозь его инопланетную структуру.


--- The Disciples of Cthulhu.txt ---

[1] Zoth L105
Berglund Overview The first edition of The Disciples of Cthulhu was released in paperback format by DAW Books in 1976. It was edited by Edward P. Berglund, featured cover art by Karel Thole, and ran to a total of 288 pages. This edition was also translated into French (as Les adorateurs de Cthulhu ) and German (as Cthulhu's Kinder In February 1996, the Call of Cthulhu roleplaying game publisher Chaosium released The Disciples of Cthulhu: Second Revised Edition as part of their Cthulhu Cycle , once again edited by Berglund, but with two of the original stories (" The Feaster from Afar " and " Zoth-Ommog ") were removed and replaced (with " Dope War of the Black Tong " and "Glimpses"). This version ran to 258 pages and featured brand new cover artwork by H. E. Fassl , with an interior plate by Earl Geier First Edition Editor's Foreword by Edward P. Berglund Introduction by Robert Bloch The Fairground Horror " by Brian Lumley "The Silence of Erika Zann" by James Wade "All-Eye" by Bob Van Laerhoven The Tugging " by Ramsey Campbell Where Yidhra Walks " by Walter C. DeBill, Jr.

--- The Horror in the Gallery.txt ---

[1] Zoth L9
НАЗВАНИЕ: The Horror in the Gallery ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:31:30 СИМВОЛОВ: 6267 This article is for the short story "Zoth-Ommog", aka "The Horror in the Gallery"; for the Great Old One see Zoth-Ommog "The Horror in the Gallery" , originally published under the title "Zoth-Ommog" in the 1976 anthology The Disciples of Cthulhu , is a Cthulhu Mythos short story by Lin Carter , and part of his Xothic Legend Cycle Great Old Ones and the Elder Gods , based on and including references to the works of several Mythos authors. Synopsis The story is presented as a police testimony given by Arthur Wilcox Hodgkins in 1929, after a night watchman is found murdered in the South Gallery of the Sanbourne Institute of Pacific Antiquities in Santiago, California. Hodgkins maintains that there were actually two bodies, the second being the body of the night watchman's murderer.

[2] Zoth L65
Hodgkins maintains that there were actually two bodies, the second being the body of the night watchman's murderer. Hodgkins worked at the Sanbourne Institute as an assistant to curator Henry Stephenson Blaine , but assumed Blaine's duties following the latter' internment in a mental institution (see " Out of the Ages "). At the time of his mental collapse, Dr. Blaine had been cataloging the collection of files and artifacts belonging to the late Professor Copeland , including a mysterious jade figurine found off the coast of Ponape that represented the god Zoth-Ommog . Since Copeland himself had died raving in an asylum , the press reacts to the news of Blaine's madness with the sensationalist claim that the haunted figurine had made another victim. Hodgkins is told to resume the cataloging, as the directors want the Copeland Collection to be put on display in the South Gallery later that year. A few months later, Hodgkins receives a call from the asylum telling him that Dr.

[3] Zoth L85
A few months later, Hodgkins receives a call from the asylum telling him that Dr. Blaine is extremely agitated, asking to see Hodgkins immediately. Blaine tells Hodgkins to destroy the Ponape figurine rather than put it on display, and advises him to consult the Necronomicon at the Miskatonic University library. He makes Hodgkins promise to read the Copeland files marked as "Xothic Legend Cycle", which Hodgkins agrees to. From Copeland's files (and Blaine's annotations), Hodgkins learns about the Cthulhu Mythos and particularly about Cthulhu 's son, Zoth-Ommog, imprisoned under the island of Ponape. That same night, he starts experiencing bad dreams in which the face of the Zoth-Ommog figurine watches him menacingly.

[4] Zoth L85
Blaine tells Hodgkins to destroy the Ponape figurine rather than put it on display, and advises him to consult the Necronomicon at the Miskatonic University library. He makes Hodgkins promise to read the Copeland files marked as "Xothic Legend Cycle", which Hodgkins agrees to. From Copeland's files (and Blaine's annotations), Hodgkins learns about the Cthulhu Mythos and particularly about Cthulhu 's son, Zoth-Ommog, imprisoned under the island of Ponape. That same night, he starts experiencing bad dreams in which the face of the Zoth-Ommog figurine watches him menacingly. Studying passages copied by Copeland from books such as De Vermis Mysteriis The Revelations of Glaaki and the Cultes des Goules , Hodgkins learns that the Great Old Ones can create psychic links with those who have been in contact with the idols and statuettes that represent them, and those who have been in contact with such objects will have their life force drained by these eldritch deities, and see them in their nightmares.

[5] Zoth L137
A new problem arises, however, because none of the occult tomes will explain what one must do with the star-stone, and Armitage and Lapham debate about whether placing it in proximity of the idol would be sufficient to negate its effects, or whether some ritual must be performed. When Hodgkins learns from newspapers that the Ponape figurine has been put on display, he returns to California as fast as possible, taking one of the star-stones with him. Hodgkins arrives at the Sanbourne Institute some hours before opening and finds the night watchman murdered and the Zoth-Ommog figurine emanating a strange energy, as if it was alive. He soon finds the intruder who had bludgeoned the watchman to death, and whose batrachian aspect, sea-salt smell and large mittens reveal as a Deep One intent on using the figurine to summon Zoth-Ommog himself.

[6] Zoth L141
When Hodgkins learns from newspapers that the Ponape figurine has been put on display, he returns to California as fast as possible, taking one of the star-stones with him. Hodgkins arrives at the Sanbourne Institute some hours before opening and finds the night watchman murdered and the Zoth-Ommog figurine emanating a strange energy, as if it was alive. He soon finds the intruder who had bludgeoned the watchman to death, and whose batrachian aspect, sea-salt smell and large mittens reveal as a Deep One intent on using the figurine to summon Zoth-Ommog himself. Hodgkins desperately tosses the star-stone at the idol, and the collision results in a blast that annihilates the two objects and releases streaks of lightning-like energy, one of which hits the Deep One and kills him by liquefying his body (leaving nothing for the police to find later). Hodgkins loses consciousness and wakes up in a hospital suffering from severe burns.

--- The Shadow Out of Space.txt ---

[1] Dark Star L73
It was published in the 1957 Arkham House collection The Survivor and Others Synopsis In Arkham , 1933, a woman named Abigail Piper seeks out psychoanalyst Nathaniel Corey in the hopes that he can help her brother Amos, an anthropologist from Miskatonic University . Three years before, Amos Piper collapsed suddenly and fell into a long period of amnesia, followed by an alteration of personality, from which he recovered only recently. However, his recovery also brought in new symptoms in the form of terrifying hallucinations and nightmares. In his dreams, Piper sees himself as a captive mind within the body of a large, rugose, cone-shaped creature with crab-like pincers, living on a planet orbiting a dark star in the Taurus constellation. He sees several other bodies like his, some hosting captive minds originating from several points in time and space. There are also many warders and one elderly instructor, who converses with the captives. From the instructor, Piper learns about the Great Race , a species which once inhabited the Earth , but got caught up in the war between the Elder Gods and the Ancient Ones and were forced to escape, first to Jupiter , then to other planets.

--- The_Family_Tree_of_the_Gods_2a73d6cc6e [The Family Tree of the Gods.txt].txt ---

[7] Ycnagnnisssz L43
Smith's chart is chiefly concerned with Tsathoggua and his close relations. Once again, Azathoth appears at the top of the chart, but so does Ycnagnnisssz, described as a "fissionary being from the dark star Zoth."

[8] Ycnagnnisssz L43
It is conceivable that Ycnagnnisssz was literally spawned by the dark star Zoth, though one doesn't typically think of stars as giving birth to sentient beings. Alternatively, Ycnagnnisssz may have been spawned near Zoth from some parent that is not specified.

[9] Ycnagnnisssz L43
Alternatively, Ycnagnnisssz may have been spawned near Zoth from some parent that is not specified.

[14] Ycnagnnisssz L59
Thus, in matters Cthulhuvian, Lovecraft's views take precedence; whereas in matters Tsathogguish, Smith's views must be taken as prime. Bearing this guideline in mind, the following combined chart suggests itself as at least a possibility: The first compromise in this chart regards Ycnagnnisssz, who here is explicitly listed as a spawn of Azathoth, though Smith did not specify Ycnagnnisssz's forbears.

[15] Ycnagnnisssz L59
Besides this, Smith describes Ycnagnnisssz as a "fissionary being," which means that it is close to the Azathothian prototype and thus probably not separated from Azathoth by many generations.

--- Ubb.txt ---

[1] Zoth L91
Ubb dwells in the cold fastness of the Pacific Ocean , with the rest of his race, the Yuggs . The Yuggs are said to guard their gods, the Great Old Ones Ythogtha and Zoth-Ommog , whose tombs, legend tells, lie in the Abyss of Yhe and at the bottom of an abyssal trench near the island of Ponape , respectively. In the first edition of Malleus Monstrorum , Ubb is described as a highly divine entity with the potential to evolve into a Great Old One in the future.

According to The Xothic Legend Cycle , it is where Cthulhu mated with Idh-yaa to beget Ghatanothoa Ythogtha , and Zoth-Ommog Xoth is also the native home of Ycnágnnisssz and Zstylzhemghi , and was the temporary home of the latter's "husband", Ghisguth , and their progeny, the infant Tsathoggua . Tsathoggua later went to live on Yuggoth . Afterward, he fled to Cykranosh to escape Cxaxukluth 's cannibalistic eating habits. According to Friedrich von Junzt , Xoth is located in the same star cluster as the planets Abbith and Ymar.

--- Xothic Legend Cycle.txt ---

[1] Zoth L43
НАЗВАНИЕ: Xothic Legend Cycle ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:35:12 СИМВОЛОВ: 7652 The Xothic Legend Cycle is a series of short stories by Lin Carter that are based on the Cthulhu Mythos of H. P. Lovecraft , primarily on Lovecraft's stories " The Call of Cthulhu " and " Out of the Aeons The cycle is centered on a trinity of deities said to be the "sons" of Cthulhu Ghatanothoa Ythogtha , and Zoth-Ommog .

[2] Zoth L81
All these stories are to be found collected, with others, in The Xothic Legend Cycle: The Complete Mythos Fiction of Lin Carter Chaosium , 1997). Originally Carter had assembled some of these stories for a volume he planned to call The Terror Out of Time . Stories to have been included in this collection included "The Dweller in the Tomb" (to have been renamed "Zanthu"); "The Winfield Inheritance"; " Zoth-Ommog " (to be renamed "The Terror Out of Time"); "Out of the Ages" and "Them From Outside." The collection was submitted to DAW Books and Arkham House but was unpublished in Carter's lifetime.

[3] Zoth L87
The collection was submitted to DAW Books and Arkham House but was unpublished in Carter's lifetime. "Zoth-Ommog" (originally titled "The Horror in the Gallery") was published in the original DAW Books edition of Edward Berglund , ed, The Disciples of Cthulhu , but was omitted from the Chaosium reprint of this anthology; however, the story appears under its original title in The Xothic Legend Cycle: The Complete Mythos Fiction of Lin Carter (Chaosium, 1997). "Them from Outside" was scheduled to appear in an issue of Crypt of Cthulhu as "Concerning Them from Outside".

[4] Zoth L123
"Them from Outside" was scheduled to appear in an issue of Crypt of Cthulhu as "Concerning Them from Outside". The cycle introduces various mythos elements, such as the Zanthu Tablets , the Ponape Scripture Father Ubb and the yuggs , and two new Great Old Ones : Ythogtha and Zoth-Ommog. The cycle also features the (fictional) Sanbourne Institute of Pacific Antiquities , perhaps Lin Carter's answer to Lovecraft's Miskatonic University . The lost continent of Mu also figures prominently in the cycle, as do the events that led to its sinking.

[5] Zoth L139

The lost continent of Mu also figures prominently in the cycle, as do the events that led to its sinking. (The Elder Gods as punishers of the Old Ones (a God and Satan theme) was not a Lovecraftian invention, but was introduced into the Mythos by August Derleth and carried on by a multitude of other Mythos authors, most notably Brian Lumley and Lin Carter In Leigh Blackmore 's sequel "The Return of Zoth-Ommog", Zoth-Ommog re-emerges on the island of Ponape and wreaks havoc until he is destroyed by an ancient ritual. Sanbourne Institute of Pacific Antiquities Main article: Sanbourne Institute of Pacific Antiquities Stories Each story is set in the early 20th century and is told from the point of view of a scholarly narrator. Each narrator in turn becomes the protagonist of the next story in the series.

[11] Zoth L285
The story consists primarily of the journal entries of Professor Copeland and details the ill-fated Copeland-Ellington Expedition into Central Asia in 1913, of which Copeland was the only survivor. "Out of the Ages" This story introduces the "Xothic Legend Cycle", a theory first proposed by Harold Hadley Copeland. The story also features the "Ponape Figurine", a sinister statuette depicting Zoth-Ommog, the Dweller in the Deeps "The Thing in the Pit" The fourth story in the cycle chronicles the events that led to the destruction of Mu. [13] Zoth L337

"The Winfield Heritance" The final story in the cycle is only loosely tied to the other four. Описание править Ycnàgnnissssz — гиперборейское имя одного из прародителей богов (другим является Азатот), согласно генеалогии в Пергаментах Пнома и связанной с ним Книге Эйбона, где говорится, что это ровесник Азатота.

[4] Зот L23
Устремившись по червоточине во вселенную Древних, вселенную Тьмы, она предстала перед пантеоном Азатота. Перемещение исказило её суть, и свет, что она несла в себе, обернулся сияющей тьмой. Йкнагннисссз превратилась в раскалённую чёрную звезду, много позже ставшую известной как звезда Зот.

[7] Зот L27
Присутствие где-то на дальнем рубеже вселенной звезды Зот также подвергается авторитетной критике современных учёных, утверждающих, что "Зот" — это всего лишь гиперборейский вариант названия звезды Ксот. Кроме того, часто встречается утверждение, что Йкнагннисссз — не более чем аллегория, введённая гиперборейскими хронистами для красочного обозначения двойственной природы Азатота.

( CIRCLE Selected Letters of Clark Ashton Smith 189) Overview Ycnàgnnisssz is a Hyperborean name for one of the progenitors of the gods ( Azathoth being the other) according to the genealogy in the Parchments of Pnom and the related Book of Eibon .

[2] Ycnágnnisssz L89
The character first appeared in Clark Ashton Smith 's 1934 " Genealogical Chart of the Elder Gods " answer to R. H. Barlow 's now missing style sheet. ( CIRCLE Selected Letters of Clark Ashton Smith 189) Overview Ycnàgnnisssz is a Hyperborean name for one of the progenitors of the gods ( Azathoth being the other) according to the genealogy in the Parchments of Pnom and the related Book of Eibon . Not much is known about the entity but Ycnágnnisssz is an Outer God from the dark star Xoth , and it spawned Zstylzhemghi by fission. It is a fissionary being who is possibly either Abhoth or Ubbo-Sathla ; however, these two names are also Hyperborean, so it may be a different entity. It might also be the Nameless Mist or the Darkness , or possibly it is the "Azathoth-Ubbo-Sathla", the bivalvular shape of Azathoth.

НАЗВАНИЕ: Yuggya Chants ИСТОЧНИК: ДАТА: 2026-06-15 03:35:24 СИМВОЛОВ: 527 Yuggya Chants Real World Information First appearance EXP Out of the Ages Created by Lin Carter The Yuggya Chants is a fictional grimoire created by Lin Carter for his Xothic Legend Cycle Prof. Harold Hadley Copeland purchased a copy of the Yuggya Chants from a Lascar sailor in San Francisco. They include an invocation to call the Yuggs from the sea: creatures that can promise knowledge, riches and astral projection to their human allies in exchange for servitude to their lords, Ythogtha and Zoth-Ommog EXP Out of the Ages


--- Yuzh (Cthulhu Universalis).txt ---

[1] Dark Star L11
НАЗВАНИЕ: Yuzh (Cthulhu Universalis) ИСТОЧНИК: Yuzh CAS Infernal ] A dark star, described as a "blackly flaming orb," that is a companion of Yamil Zacra and a contrast to the other's "insupportable whiteness." The planet Pnidleethon revolves around both. Viewed from Earth, the system would appear to be between Algol and Polaris , though it is not clear whether Yuzh is actually visible. Avalzant told the evil aspirant Oliver Woadley that he must pledge himself to Yuzh, Yamil Zacra, and Pnidleethon. The hypnotized victims, even while feeling terrified and revolted, are incapable of offering any resistance to the amorphous enchantress. [31] Zoth L8993
Volks, and it should be noted some other scholars, holds that Cthylla is one of four "children" (the others being Zoth- Ommog , Ghatanothoa, and Ythogtha).

[99] Zoth L17391
According to the Ponape Scripture, Idh-yaa joined with Cthulhu to produce the entities known as Cthylla, Zoth- Ommog , and Ghatanothoa.

[296] Zoth L38539
Other names: Deep Dweller, Ott’mogg. Zoth-Ommog is believed to have been spawned by Great Cthulhu after that entity’s arrival from Xoth.

[297] Zoth L38551
Common to all is the belief that Zoth-Ommog dwells in the deep oceans, but whether it is bound within the remains of the corpse city R’lyeh or free to roam is uncertain.

[298] Zoth L38561
While primarily sea-based, Zoth-Ommog appears able to transport itself on land while also capable of levitation or flight.

[299] Zoth L38585
Stories from the Pacific Ocean regarding monstrous deep-sea dwellers, often attributed to prehistoric survivors (such as Carcharocles megalodon) and other "sea monsters," may have some currency as evidence of partial sightings of Zoth-Ommog .

[300] Zoth L38599
Such concerns give rise to suspicions to the work of the Cthulhu Cult, who may have played some role in possibly freeing Zoth-Ommog as among the first steps in their goal to raise R’lyeh and their lord Cthulhu.

[301] Zoth L38605
Cult The Cult of Cthulhu holds Zoth-Ommog within their thoughts and rites as a blessed creation of their lord and master.

[302] Zoth L38611
Thus, idols and prayers to this entity may be found among cult paraphernalia, and the cult may seek to know Cthulhu through Zoth-Ommog.

[303] Zoth L38613
Some sub-sects of the cult are believed to have become solely focused on Zoth-Ommog, a being they see as the heir to Cthulhu, and a more tangible and real presence in the world.

[304] Zoth L38617
This Cult of Zoth-Ommog is known for the creation of idols that hold a power that may be used to assist in the summoning of their deity, calling forth its mind to temporarily inhabit a host body so it may deliver "guidance" to the cult’s members.

[305] Zoth L38627
It is thought that communities of deep ones offer sacrifices to Zoth-Ommog alongside Cthulhu.

[306] Zoth L38645
Encounters Seafaring folk may catch a glimpse of Zoth-Ommog in the ocean or, unfortunately, find their ship attacked and sinking, bringing them face-to-face with this entity.

[307] Zoth L38653
Likewise, divers of wrecks and ancient sunken temples may come upon strange marks made by or idols of Zoth-Ommog.

[308] Zoth L38661
Aura Idols of Zoth-Ommog feel cold to the touch and often make the holder or viewer feel as though they were momentarily sinking in deep water or, worse, drowning. While the effect is unpleasant, it seems to pass quickly.

[309] Zoth L38669
While the effect is unpleasant, it seems to pass quickly. Like its sire, Zoth-Ommog ’s presence brings a deep and unsettling loathing to most; a sense of the "other" and of things beyond human comprehension.

[310] Zoth L38707
Powers Vibrating Hum: Zoth-Ommog emits a constant humming sound from within its mass that causes humans to be perceptible to delusions.

[311] Zoth L38717

O HAGARG RYONIS Thou Lizard Who Keeps The Worm from the House The Locust from the Harvest Give aid to Thy servant! In the Circle of the Horizon There is no beast or bird That calls this wasteland home But only Thy creatures Cool and cunning Make me cool as the lizard Make me cunning as the snake O HAGARG RYONIS Thou Turtle Who Swims Ancient and Unseen In the Wisdom of Aeons Give me share of the shade Give me share of the hidden water That keeps Thy creatures living O HAGARG RYONIS Thou Dragon Who Pursues The Tyrant to His Fall The Dark Star to Its Pyre Grant Home and Hearth Grant Water and Food To the Servant of Thy Vengeance! Now, if burning Sun and parched throat had given sincerity to his prayer, then haply the Scaled Lady would make him aware of water and shade hereabout, if it be only enough for a lizard.

Они были, как он понял, заключены в тюрьмы на разных участках океанского дна океана — Ктулху в затонувшем каменном городе Р'льех, его сын Йтхогта в Йхе, Зот-Оммог в подводной пропасти возле "Острова каменных городов", (термин этот, который по какой-то причине снова, возможно, интуиция помогла вспомнить Хоскинсу, относился к острову Понапе с его колоссальными развалинами Нан-Матала) и Гхатанотоа в запечатанном склепе в погруженной на дно горе Йаддит-Гхо.

[508] Зот L103

[545] Зот L125

Гимн Белому Огню Гимн Белому Огню Гимн Белому Огню Гимн Белому Огню Белый Огонь это Мысли Владык. Белый Огонь это Их Веления. Если захочешь — можешь их знать. Но как бы ни хотел — ослушаться не можешь. И я простираю руку, и я опускаю в себя это, желанное мной, повеление слышать!

Во всяком случае, ключевая цитата была из этого так называемого Писания Понапе, которую я запишу здесь: Легенды Ксотика 530 Что касается Гатанотоа, Твари на Горе, Он и Его Братья, Итхогта, Мерзость в Бездне, и Зот - Оммог, Обитатель Глубин, суть Сыны могучего Ктулху, Владыки Водной Бездны и грозного и ужасного Повелителя затонувшего Р'льеха; и, подобно Их Ужасному Родите лю, Который еще вернется в грядущее время, Они имеют Свою Власть над великой рыбой и змеями Глубин, и Они также заточены под ужасным заклятием Знака Старших за то, что осмелились бросить вызов Им Из Глю - Вхо за владение Землей.
